You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n    30→\n    31→## Decoder Task\n    32→\n    33→Temporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n    34→\n    35→### Decoder Inputs:\n    36→- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    37→\n    38→### Decoder Outputs\n    39→- **Lick direction** (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\n    40→- **Behavioral context** (WC = 0, DR = 1, per-trial)\n    41→- **Outcome** (incorrect = 0, correct = 1, per-trial)\n    42→- **Tongue velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    43→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    44→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    45→- **Paw velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    46→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    47→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    48→- **Motion energy**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    49→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    50→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    51→\n    52→## Target Data Format\n    53→\n    54→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    55→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    56→\n    57→```python\n    58→data = {\n    59→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    60→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    61→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    62→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    66→            ...\n    67→        ],\n    68→        ...\n    69→    ],\n    70→\n    71→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    72→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    73→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    74→            ...\n    75→        ],\n    76→        ...\n    77→    ],\n    78→\n    79→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    80→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    81→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    82→            ...\n    83→        ],\n    84→        ...\n    85→    ],\n    86→\n    87→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    88→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    89→\n    90→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    91→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    92→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    93→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    94→\n    95→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    96→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    97→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    98→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    99→        ...\n   100→    ],\n   101→\n   102→    'metadata': {\n   103→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   104→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   105→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   106→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   107→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   108→        ...\n   109→    }\n   110→}\n   111→```\n   112→\n   113→### Key Format Requirements\n   114→\n   115→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   116→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   117→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   118→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   119→\n   120→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   121→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   122→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   123→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   124→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   125→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   126→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   127→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   128→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   129→\n   130→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   131→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   132→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   133→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   134→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   135→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   136→\n   137→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   138→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   139→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   140→\n   141→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   142→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   143→\n   144→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   146→\n   147→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   148→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   149→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   150→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   151→\n   152→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   153→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   154→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   155→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   156→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   157→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   158→\n   159→## Python environment\n   160→\n   161→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   162→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   163→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   164→\n   165→## Decoder Reference\n   166→\n   167→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   168→- Optional arguments:\n   169→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   170→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   171→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   172→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   173→\n   174→### Purpose\n   175→\n   176→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   177→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   178→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   179→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   180→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   181→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   182→\n   183→### Consistency requirements\n   184→\n   185→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   186→- Loading of data\n   187→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   188→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   189→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   190→\n   191→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   192→\n   193→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   194→\n   195→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   196→\n   197→Carefully verify your work after every step. Which variables contain neural activity?\n   320→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   321→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   322→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   323→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   324→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   325→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   326→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   327→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   328→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   329→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   330→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   331→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   332→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   333→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   334→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   335→\n   336→**Done when**:\n   337→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   338→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   339→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   340→\n   341→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   342→\n   343→---\n   344→\n   345→### Step 6: Script Development\n   346→\n   347→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   348→\n   349→**Actions**:\n   350→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   351→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   352→- Include options:\n   353→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   354→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   355→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   356→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   357→- Write efficient code:\n   358→  - Vectorize loops\n   359→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   360→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   361→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   362→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   363→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   364→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   365→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   366→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   367→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   368→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   369→\n   370→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   371→\n   372→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   373→\n   374→---\n   375→\n   376→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   377→\n   378→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   379→\n   380→**Actions**:\n   381→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   382→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   383→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   384→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   385→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   386→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   387→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   388→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   389→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   390→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   391→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   392→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   393→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   394→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   395→\n   396→**Done when**: \n   397→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   398→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   399→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   400→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   401→\n   402→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   403→\n   404→---\n   405→\n   406→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   407→\n   408→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   409→\n   410→**Actions**:\n   411→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   412→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   413→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   414→\n   415→**Investigate any issues**:\n   416→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   417→\n   418→**Done when**: \n   419→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   420→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   421→- Accuracies are above chance\n   422→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   423→\n   424→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   425→\n   426→---\n   427→\n   428→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   429→\n   430→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   431→\n   432→**Actions**:\n   433→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   434→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   435→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   436→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   437→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   438→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   439→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   440→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   441→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   442→\n   443→**Done when**:\n   444→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   445→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   446→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   447→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   448→\n   449→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   450→\n   451→---\n   452→\n   453→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   454→\n   455→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   456→\n   457→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   458→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   459→\n   460→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   461→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   462→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   463→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   464→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   465→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   466→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   467→\n   468→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   469→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   470→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   471→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   472→- Fix any mismatches. \n   473→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   474→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   475→\n   476→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   477→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   478→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   479→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   480→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   481→\n   482→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   483→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   484→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   485→\n   486→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   487→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   488→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   489→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   490→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   491→\n   492→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   493→\n   494→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   495→\n   496→---\n   497→\n   498→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   499→\n   500→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   501→\n   502→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   503→\n   504→**Actions**:\n   505→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   506→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   507→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   508→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n   509→\n   510→**Done when**: \n   511→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   512→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   513→\n   514→---\n   515→\n   516→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   517→\n   518→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   519→\n   520→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   521→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   522→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   523→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   524→\n   525→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n   526→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n   527→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n   528→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   529→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   530→\n   531→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   532→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   533→\n   534→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   535→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   536→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   537→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   538→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   539→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n   540→\n   541→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   542→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   543→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   544→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   545→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   546→\n   547→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   548→\n   549→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   550→\n   551→---\n   552→\n   553→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   554→\n   555→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   556→\n   557→**Actions**:\n   558→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   559→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   560→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   561→  - Validation results and tables\n   562→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   563→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   564→  - Dataset description\n   565→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   566→  - Output format specification\n   567→  - Key statistics\n   568→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   569→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   570→\n   571→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   572→\n   573→---\n   574→\n   575→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   576→\n   577→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   829→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   830→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   831→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   832→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   833→\n   834→### Computational Efficiency\n   835→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   836→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   837→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   838→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   839→\n   840→## Success Criteria\n   841→\n   842→A successful conversion should:\n   843→1. Match the target data structure exactly\n   844→2. Preserve all relevant information from the source\n   845→3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n   846→4. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- **Lick direction** (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\n- **Behavioral context** (WC = 0, DR = 1, per-trial)\n- **Outcome** (incorrect = 0, correct = 1, per-trial)\n- **Tongue velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n- **Paw velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n- **Motion energy**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. Optional[int]:\n   350→    feat_names = view.get(\"featNames\", [])\n   351→    for trial_feat_names in feat_names:\n   352→        names = normalize_feat_names(trial_feat_names)\n   353→        for dict,\n   361→    view_index_one_based: int,\n   362→    feat_name: str,\n   363→    time_centers: np.ndarray,\n   364→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   365→    vidshift: float,\n   366→) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   367→    view = obj[\"traj\"][view_index_one_based - 1]\n   368→    n_trials = int(round(float(obj[\"bp\"][\"Ntrials\"])))\n   369→    feat_index = dtype=np.float32)\n   372→    if feat_index is None:\n   373→        return xpos, ypos\n   374→\n   375→    taxis = time_centers + ADVANCE_MOVEMENT\n   376→\n   377→    for trial in range(n_trials):\n   378→        trial_view = get_view_trial(view, trial)\n   379→        dropped = trial_view.get(\"NdroppedFrames\")\n   380→        if dropped is not None:\n   381→            dropped_arr = np.asarray(dropped)\n   382→            if dropped_arr.size and np.isnan(np.asarray(dropped_arr, dtype=float)).all():\n   383→                continue\n   384→\n   385→        ts = float(align_times[trial])\n   394→\n   395→        interp = interp1d(\n   396→            old_time,\n   397→            xy,\n   398→            axis=0,\n   399→            kind=\"linear\",\n   400→            bounds_error=False,\n   401→            fill_value=np.nan,\n   402→            assume_sorted=True,\n   403→        )\n   404→        xy_aligned = interp(taxis)\n   405→        xpos[:, trial] = xy_aligned[:, 0]\n   406→        ypos[:, trial] = xy_aligned[:, 1]\n   407→\n   408→        if \"tongue\" not in feat_name:\n   409→            xpos[:, trial] = fill_nearest_1d(xpos[:, trial])\n   410→            ypos[:, trial] = fill_nearest_1d(ypos[:, trial])\n   411→\n   412→    return xpos, ypos\n   413→\n   414→\n   415→def feature_velocity(xpos: np.ndarray, ypos: np.ndarray, feat_name: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   416→    xvel = np.full_like(xpos, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   417→    yvel = np.full_like(ypos, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   418→    for trial in range(xpos.shape[1]):\n   419→        tsinterp = np.column_stack([xpos[:, trial], ypos[:, trial]]).astype(np.float32)\n   420→        diffs = np.diff(tsinterp, axis=0)\n   421→        if diffs.size == 0 or np.isnan(diffs).all():\n   422→            base = np.array([0.0, 0.0], dtype=np.float32)\n   423→        else:\n   424→            base = np.nanmedian(diffs, axis=0)\n   425→        xv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0]).astype(np.float32)\n   426→        yv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1]).astype(np.float32)\n   427→        if \"tongue\" not in feat_name:\n   428→            xv = xv - base[0]\n   429→            yv = yv - base[1]\n   430→            xv = fill_nearest_1d(xv)\n   431→            yv = fill_nearest_1d(yv)\n   432→        else:\n   433→            xv = np.nan_to_num(xv, nan=0.0)\n   434→            yv = np.nan_to_num(yv, nan=0.0)\n   435→        xvel[:, trial] = xv\n   436→        yvel[:, trial] = yv\n   437→    return xvel, yvel\n   438→\n   439→\n   440→def aggregate_speed(\n   441→    obj: dict,\n   442→    feature_map: Dict[int, List[str]],\n   443→    time_centers: np.ndarray,\n   444→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   445→    vidshift: float,\n   446→) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, List[str]]:\n   447→    speed_components = []\n   448→    used_features: List[str] = []\n   449→    for view_index, feat_names in feature_map.items():\n   450→        for feat_name in feat_names:\n   451→            xpos, ypos = aligned_position(obj, view_index, feat_name, time_centers, align_times, vidshift)\n   452→            if not np.isfinite(xpos).any() and not np.isfinite(ypos).any():\n   453→                continue\n   454→            xvel, yvel = feature_velocity(xpos, ypos, feat_name)\n   455→            speed = np.sqrt(np.square(xvel) + np.square(yvel))\n   456→            speed_components.append(speed)\n   457→            used_features.append(f\"view{view_index}:{feat_name}\")\n   458→    if not speed_components:\n   459→        n_trials = int(round(float(obj[\"bp\"][\"Ntrials\"])))\n   460→        return np.zeros((time_centers.size, n_trials), dtype=np.float32), used_features\n   461→    stacked = np.stack(speed_components, axis=2)\n   462→    valid = np.isfinite(stacked)\n   463→    count = np.sum(valid, axis=2)\n   464→    summed = np.sum(np.where(valid, stacked, 0.0), axis=2)\n   465→    agg = np.divide(summed, np.maximum(count, 1), dtype=np.float32)\n   466→    agg[count == 0] = np.nan\n   467→    agg = np.nan_to_num(agg, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0).astype(np.float32)\n   468→    return agg, used_features\n   469→\n   470→\n   471→def unwrap_embedded_motion_energy(raw_me) -> List[np.ndarray]:\n   472→    data = raw_me\n   473→    seen = set()\n   474→    while isinstance(data, dict) and 566→    out[order[flat.size // 2 :]] = 1\n   567→    return out.reshape(traces.shape)\n   568→\n   569→\n   570→def build_trial_mask(obj: dict) -> np.ndarray:\n   571→    bp = obj[\"bp\"]\n   572→ squeeze = False\n    if x.ndim == 1:\n        x = x[:, None]\n        squeeze = True\n\n    if bctype == \"reflect\":\n        x_filt = np.concatenate([x[:n, :], x], axis=0)\n        trim = n\n    elif bctype == \"zeropad\":\n        x_filt = np.concatenate([np.zeros((n, x.shape[1]), dtype=x.dtype), x], axis=0)\n        trim = n\n    else:\n        x_filt = x\n        trim = 0\n\n    kern = gaussian_window(n)\n    kern[: n // 2] = 0.0\n    kern /= kern.sum()\n\n    out = np.empty_like(x_filt, dtype=np.float64)\n    for col in range(x_filt.shape[1]):\n        out[:, col] = np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float32).copy()\n    mask = np.isnan(x)\n    if not mask.any():\n        return x\n    valid = np.flatnonzero(~mask)\n    if valid.size == 0:\n        return x\n    idx = np.arange(x.size)\n    pos = np.searchsorted(valid, idx)\n    left = valid[np.clip(pos - 1, 0, valid.size - 1)]\n    right = valid[np.clip(pos, 0, valid.size - 1)]\n    choose_left = np.abs(idx - left) <= np.abs(right - idx)\n    nearest = np.where(choose_left, left, right)\n    x[mask] = x[nearest[mask]]\n    return x\n\n\ndef normalize_feat_names(raw_feat_names) -> alignment logic. |\n    48→\n    49→### Notes\n    50→- The reference data object is a MATLAB struct `obj` with trial/task data in `obj.bp`, spike data in `obj.clu`, DLC trajectories in `obj.traj`, and sometimes motion energy in `obj.me`.\n    51→- The main ephys processing path is `loadSessionData -> processData -> findTrials/findClusters -> alignSpikes -> getSeq -> removeLowFRClusters -> baselineFR`.\n    52→- Reference analyses commonly use `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`, which matches the decoder task requirement.\n    53→- Spike data are not converted to dF/F; these are electrophysiology sessions. Neural activity is processed as binned/smoothed firing rates.\n    54→- Neural binning is configurable. Core loaders support arbitrary `dt`; multiple figure scripts use `dt = 1/100` (10 ms) and some use `dt = 1/200` (5 ms). Final choice must be reconciled with paper/methods and data in later steps.\n    55→- `getSeq` bins spikes on `[tmin, tmax)` using `histc`, then smooths counts converted to Hz with `mySmooth`. Session-level single-trial neural data end up as `(time, neurons, trials)`.\n    56→- Cluster curation has two layers:\n    57→  - Quality filter by label using `findClusters`.\n    58→  - Firing-rate filter using `removeLowFRClusters`, typically `lowFR = 1` Hz in several figure scripts and tutorial code.\n    59→- Video/behavior streams are interpolated to the neural/session time axis rather than used at native frame rate.\n    60→- Motion energy alignment is explicit: `interp1(frameTimes - video_offset - align_event_time, me.data{trial}, obj.time + advance_movement)`, then edge NaNs are filled with nearest values.\n    61→- DLC trajectories also use frame times corrected by a 0.5 s or computed video offset depending on loader/helper path. This is important for later consistency checks on temporal alignment.\n    62→- Trial selection is driven by expressions over `obj.bp` fields such as `R`, `L`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `autowater`, `stim.enable`, and `early`.\n    63→- `autowater` is the code’s proxy for behavioral context: delayed response / 2AFC (`autowater == 0`) versus water-cued (`autowater == 1`).\n    64→- Choice/context decoding scripts in `code/ChoiceContextDecoding` balance trials across classes, typically use hit/miss subsets, and operate on `obj.trialdat` or interpolated kinematic tensors aligned to go cue.\n    65→- Metadata loaders encode the included sessions and which probe corresponds to ALM. Some sessions have one relevant probe and some analyses can concatenate two probes within a session.\n    66→\n    67→---\n    68→\n    69→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    70→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    71→\n    72→### Data Structure\n    73→- Top-level raw-data folders:\n    74→  - `data/Ephys_Behavior`: delayed-response / water-cued sessions with neural data and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n    75→  - `data/RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior`: randomized-delay sessions; “25 sessions, nine mice”; “19 sessions… four mice” |\n   122→| Sessions / subject | Two-context mean 2.0; fixed-delay DR mean about 2.8; randomized-delay mean about 4.8 | Computed from paper totals above |\n   123→| Trials (total) | Not explicitly tabulated in text | Not numerically stated in methods/paper text provided |\n   124→| Trials / session | Not explicitly tabulated in text; block structure is described | “approximately 100 DR trials” at session start, then “10–25 trials” per interleaved block |\n   125→| Neural data time bin | 5 ms in published decoding analyses | “each bin is 5 ms” |\n   126→| Behavior data time bin | 400 Hz raw video, approximately 2.5 ms per frame; decoding analyses also operate on 5 ms bins | “High-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate)” and “each bin is 5 ms” |\n   127→| Reward rate | Reward delivered for correct DR choices; WC reward is approximately 3 µl at a random port | “water reward was delivered” / “an approximately 3-µl water reward was presented randomly” |\n   128→| Task/behavior statistic 1 | Fixed-delay DR performance criterion: >70% accuracy before/for expert animals | “reached at least 70% accuracy”; “became experts (>70% accuracy …)” |\n   129→| Task/behavior statistic 2 | Randomized-delay early-lick criterion: <20% | “became experts (>70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate)” |\n   130→| Task/behavior statistic 3 | Behavioral analysis sessions required at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction | “at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction… and 20 correct WC trials for each direction” |\n   131→\n   132→\n   133→### Processing Details\n   134→- Task structure:\n   135→  - DR task: 1.3 s auditory sample, then delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue.\n   136→  - Standard fixed-delay DR task used 0.9 s delay for 12 mice; one mouse had 0.7 s delay that was linearly time-warped to 0.9 s in analyses.\n   137→  - WC task omits auditory cues; a water drop appears at a random side/time.\n   138→  - Two-context sessions begin with about 100 DR trials, then alternate WC/DR blocks of 10-25 trials; sessions begin in DR.\n   139→  - Randomized-delay task uses six delay values: 0.3, 0.6, 1.2, 1.8, 2.4, 3.6 s.\n   140→- Temporal alignment:\n   141→  - Methods and figure text repeatedly describe activity, movement, and decoding relative to the go cue (or water drop in WC plots); the user task specifically requires go-cue alignment, matching the main neural alignment used in the code and paper.\n   142→  - ITI context analyses use the 300 ms before sample onset.\n   143→- Kinematic/video processing:\n   144→  - Two 400 Hz cameras.\n   145→  - Tongue, jaw, nose tracked in both views; paws tracked from bottom view only.\n   146→  - Use x/y position and velocity per tracked feature.\n   147→  - Missing values are filled with nearest values for all features except the tongue.\n   148→  - Tongue angle and length are derived from the bottom camera.\n   149→- Motion energy processing:\n   150→  - Frame-wise motion energy is based on the absolute difference between the median of the next five frames and previous five frames.\n   151→  - Each frame is summarized by the 99th percentile across pixels.\n   152→  - Move/non-move threshold is set manually per session from bimodal distributions.\n   153→- Decoding in the paper:\n   154→  - Equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials were used for choice decoding.\n   155→  - Separate models were trained at each time bin for neural and kinematic decoding.\n   156→  - Models used ridge regularization and four-fold cross-validation.\n   157→  - Chance accuracy was computed by shuffling labels.\n   158→- Neural recording/processing:\n   159→  - ALM extracellular recordings from H2 and Neuropixels 1.0 probes.\n   160→  - Spike sorting via JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 with manual Phy curation.\n   161→  - Sessions were included only if they had at least 10 units.\n   162→  - For most analyses, all units with firing rate >1 Hz were included.\n   163→  - For subspace alignment and some single-unit analyses, only well-isolated single units with firing rate >1 Hz were included.\n   164→\n   165→### Curation Steps\n   166→\n   167→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   168→- Manual spike-sorting curation distinguishes well-isolated single units from multiunits.\n   169→- Recording sessions must have at least 10 units to be analyzed.\n   170→- A >1 Hz firing-rate threshold is applied.\n   171→- Some analyses use only well-isolated single units >1 Hz; other analyses include all units >1 Hz.\n   172→\n   173→**Trial curation rules**:\n   174→- Early-lick trials are omitted from analyses.\n   175→- Ignore / no-response trials are omitted from behavioral analyses.\n   176→- Behavioral analyses require enough correct trials per class: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction.\n   177→- Choice decoding uses equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials.\n   178→\n   179→### Decoders Trained\n   180→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   181→| Choice from uninstructed movement / neural activity | Reported qualitatively as above chance over time; exact numeric curve values are shown graphically, not tabulated in text |\n   182→| Context from kinematics / neural activity | Reported qualitatively as above chance over time, including during ITI; exact numeric curve values are shown graphically, not tabulated in text |\n   183→\n   184→---\n   185→\n   186→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   187→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   188→\n   189→### Discrepancies Found\n   190→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   191→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   192→| Randomized-delay session count | `Figure3i.m` loads JEB11 (2), JEB12 (2), JEB23 (7; `2023-10-20` commented out), JEB24 (8) = 19 sessions | Raw folder has 22 files; 2 (`JEB24_2023-10-03`, `JEB24_2023-10-04`) have no `clu`, leaving 20 neural-capable files | “19 sessions… four mice” | Resolved: the analyzed randomized-delay set is the 19 sessions explicitly selected by the loader files, not every raw file in the folder. |\n   193→| Fixed-delay DR session/mouse count | `Figure3d.m` / `Figure3f.m` load 25 sessions spanning 10 subject IDs (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB13`, `JEB14`, `JEB15`, `JEB19`) | Raw `Ephys_Behavior` folder contains exactly those 25 sessions from 10 mice | “25 sessions, nine mice” | Partially resolved: session count is consistent at 25, but mouse count differs by one between paper text and released code/data. Treat this as a likely text/reporting discrepancy; follow the explicit session lists in code. |\n   194→| Two-context dataset size | `Figure8a_thru_c.m` / `Figure8d.m` load 12 sessions from 7 subject IDs (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB19`) | Raw fixed-delay folder contains 22 sessions with both `autowater` states present | “12 sessions, six mice, 522 units” | Resolved for session count but not mouse count: use the 12-session subset defined in the code for context analyses. The six-vs-seven mouse discrepancy is left as a text/code inconsistency. |\n   195→| Neuron totals | Core loaders start from all clusters, then apply quality and low-FR filters; specific analyses may further restrict to well-isolated single units >1 Hz | Raw neural files contain 14,297 clusters across 45 neural sessions | Paper reports 522 units (two-context), 1,651 units (fixed-delay DR total), 845 units (randomized delay) | Resolved: raw release contains unfiltered clusters and extra sessions/files; paper counts are post-curation analysis subsets, not raw file totals. |\n   196→| Region labels | Loader files explicitly pick the ALM probe for most sessions, and `loadSessionData` concatenates both probes when `meta.probe = [1 2]` | Raw `ex.probe.loc` strings are often missing, but can include non-ALM labels on pooled sessions (for example `JEB15_2022-07-26` has `L ALM` and `L M1TJ`) | Paper emphasizes ALM recordings, but the released session metadata can include additional probe locations in pooled sessions | Resolved: preserve per-neuron region labels from raw metadata for the reference-selected probe(s), rather than forcing every neuron to `ALM`. |\n   197→| Time bin size | Code examples use both 10 ms (`dt=1/100`) and 5 ms (`dt=1/200`) depending on figure/script | Raw video is about 400 Hz (`~2.5 ms`) and spikes are stored as event times | Paper’s decoding methods explicitly state 5 ms bins | Resolved: for decoder-matched conversion, prioritize 5 ms bins over generic 10 ms examples. |\n   198→| Motion-energy availability | Code expects external `motionEnergy_*.mat` for ephys and embedded `obj.me` for behavior-only sessions | Two randomized-delay files (`JEB24_2023-10-03`, `JEB24_2023-10-04`) lack both `clu` and external motion energy | Paper describes motion energy as available for movement analyses | Resolved: exclude sessions without neural data from the decoder dataset; missing motion energy on those two sessions is not relevant once excluded. |\n   199→\n   200→---\n   201→\n   202→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   203→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   204→\n   205→### Variable Mapping\n   206→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   207→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   208→| `obj.clu{probe}.trialtm`, `obj.clu{probe}.trial`, `obj.bp.ev.goCue` | `neural` | Align each spike to go cue, bin at 5 ms from `-2.5` to `+2.5` s, smooth with the reference kernel, convert to firing rate, arrange as `(neurons, time)` per trial | `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `removeLowFRClusters` | Use the probe(s) selected by the reference loader files; concatenate probes within a session if code does so. |\n   209→| Common aligned time axis | `input[0]` | Continuous time-from-go-cue series, same for every trial, shape `(1, time)` | `getSeq` / paper decoding methods | Input name: `time_from_go_cue`. |\n   210→| `obj.bp.R` / `obj.bp.L` | `output[0]` | Per-trial categorical label: left=`0`, right=`1` | `findTrials`, task definitions in methods | Exclude early and no-response trials so choice/outcome are well-defined. |\n   211→| `obj.bp.autowater` | `output[1]` | Per-trial categorical label remapped to WC=`0`, DR=`1` | `findTrials`; paper/task description | Raw code uses `autowater==1` as WC. |\n   212→| `obj.bp.hit` / `obj.bp.miss` | `output[2]` | Per-trial categorical label: miss=`0`, hit=`1` | `getOutcome` | Ignore/no-response trials excluded rather than forced into incorrect. |\n   213→| DLC trajectories for tongue-related features | `output[3]` | Reference kinematic interpolation and velocity computation, then aggregate to a single tongue-speed trace and bin by per-session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematics` | Because the decoder spec needs one tongue-velocity output, aggregate across tongue velocity channels after reference processing. |\n   214→| DLC trajectories for paw-related features | `output[4]` | Reference kinematic interpolation and velocity computation, then aggregate to a single paw-speed trace and bin by per-session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematics` | Paws are tracked in the bottom view only. |\n   215→| Motion energy traces (`motionEnergy_*.mat` or embedded `obj.me`) | `output[5]` | Align/interpolate to neural time axis using reference logic, then bin by per-session median | `loadMotionEnergy`, `loadMotionEnergy_Behav` | The paper’s manual move threshold is not used because the decoder task explicitly asks for 50th-percentile binning. |\n   216→\n   217→### Key Decisions\n   218→1. **Session inclusion**: Include only neural sessions represented in the reference ephys loaders.\n   219→   Fixed-delay set: all 25 sessions in `Ephys_Behavior`.\n   220→   Randomized-delay set: the 19-session subset encoded by `loadJEB11_ALMVideo`, `loadJEB12_ALMVideo`, `loadJEB23_ALMVideo`, and `loadJEB24_ALMVideo`.\n   221→   Behavior-only inhibition sessions are excluded because they have no neural activity.\n   222→2. **Trial inclusion**: Use only control/non-stimulation trials with valid behavioral labels.\n   223→   Exclude `stim.enable`, `early`, and `no` trials to match the reference analyses and keep output definitions unambiguous.\n   224→3. **Neural curation**: Apply the released-code quality filter (`all` except garbage/noisy-like labels), then remove units with firing rate `<= 1 Hz`, and exclude sessions with fewer than 10 remaining units.\n   225→4. **Temporal representation**: Use go-cue alignment and a common 5 ms bin width over `[-2.5 s, +2.5 s)`.\n   226→   This follows the paper’s explicit decoding-bin specification while preserving the baseline window used in the methods and the code.\n   227→5. **Context encoding**: Represent behavioral context as WC=`0`, DR=`1`, even though raw `autowater` uses the opposite polarity.\n   228→6. **Outcome encoding**: Represent only miss versus hit.\n   229→   Ignore trials are removed instead of being merged into incorrect.\n   230→7. **Kinematic scalar outputs**: After reproducing the reference video interpolation and per-feature velocity calculation, collapse the multi-feature representation to scalar tongue-speed and paw-speed traces using aggregate speed across the relevant tracked points.\n   231→   This is a required deviation from the reference feature set because the decoder task asks for exactly one tongue-velocity output and one paw-velocity output.\n   232→8. **Motion-energy binning**: Use the reference aligned continuous trace, but discretize with the task-mandated per-session median instead of the paper’s manually chosen move threshold.\n   233→9. **Brain-region labels**: Store per-neuron labels from the raw selected probe metadata, preserving hemisphere-specific ALM labels and any additional locations present in pooled sessions; fall back to `ALM` only when the raw location string is missing.\n   234→\n   235→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   236→- [ ] Neural spot-check: for selected session/trial/neuron, verify converted binned firing rates match raw spike times aligned to go cue and smoothed with the same kernel.\n   237→- [ ] Input check: verify `input[0]` exactly matches the converted time axis for every session/trial.\n   238→- [ ] Trial-label check: compare converted lick direction, context, and outcome against raw `bp` fields for at least three manually chosen trials in multiple sessions.\n   239→- [ ] Motion-energy alignment check: interpolate raw motion-energy traces directly from the source files for selected trials and verify equality with converted continuous traces before discretization.\n   240→- [ ] Kinematic check: recompute tongue/paw continuous speed from raw DLC trajectories for selected trials/timepoints and verify agreement with the conversion code before median-threshold binning.\n   241→- [ ] Session-count check: verify converted sessions equal the reference loader-defined neural session lists (25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay = 44 sessions).\n   242→\n Dataset Conversion Notes\n\n## Overview\n- **Dataset**: Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse (local project files: `paper.pdf`, `methods.txt`, `code/`, `data/`)\n- **Date started**: 2026-03-21\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `.manifest`\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`\n- COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `loadObjs` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadObjs.m` | LOADING | Loads each session `obj` `.mat` file and normalizes missing struct fields (`meta`, `ex`, `me`). |\n| `findDataFn` | `code/utils/findDataFn.m` | LOADING | Resolves the `data_structure_*` filename for a given animal/date. |\n| `loadJEB*_ALMVideo`, `loadJGR*_ALMVideo`, etc. | `code/DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/*.m` | LOADING | Enumerate included ephys+video sessions and assign the relevant probe number(s). |\n| `loadSessionData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadSessionData.m` | LOADING | Main reference entry point: loops sessions/probes, calls `processData`, then concatenates across probes into session-level outputs. |\n| `processData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Performs trial selection, cluster selection, spike alignment, PSTH/trial matrix creation, low-FR curation, and baseline statistics. |\n| `findTrials` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates logical expressions on `obj.bp` fields to produce per-condition trial indices. |\n| `findClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | Selects clusters by quality label; `'all'` excludes `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, and `real?`. |\n| `alignSpikes` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | PROCESSING | Aligns per-spike trial times to the chosen event (`goCue`, `moveOnset`, `firstLick`, `lastLick`, `jawOnset`). |\n| `getSeq` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | Bins aligned spikes from `tmin:dt:tmax`, smooths them, and creates `obj.psth{probe}` and `obj.trialdat{probe}`. |\n| `removeLowFRClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Removes neurons with mean firing rate below `lowFR` using the trial-averaged PSTH. |\n| `baselineFR` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m` | PROCESSING | Computes baseline firing rate and sigma from `-0.5 s` to `sample` onset. |\n| `findVideoOffset` | `code/funcs/findVideoOffset.m` | PROCESSING | Computes offset between neural and video file starts for frame-time alignment. |\n| `loadMotionEnergy` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m` | PROCESSING | Loads session motion energy, interpolates it onto the neural time axis aligned to `params.alignEvent`, fills edge NaNs, and thresholds into move/non-move. |\n| `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m` | PROCESSING | Interpolates DLC coordinates to the aligned trial time axis and derives x/y displacement and velocity per feature. |\n| `findVelocity` | `code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | Computes per-trial velocity from interpolated trajectories; non-tongue features are baseline-subtracted, tongue NaNs become zero velocity. |\n| `getKinematics` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematics.m` | PROCESSING | Builds the full behavioral feature tensor by combining DLC kinematics, derived tongue angle/length, and motion energy. |\n| `loadBehavSessionData` | `code/funcs/fig3/loadBehavSessionData.m` | LOADING | Behavior-only/session helper that finds trial IDs and video offset; includes `firstLick` alignment logic. |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference data object is a MATLAB struct `obj` with trial/task data in `obj.bp`, spike data in `obj.clu`, DLC trajectories in `obj.traj`, and sometimes motion energy in `obj.me`.\n- The main ephys processing path is `loadSessionData -> processData -> findTrials/findClusters -> alignSpikes -> getSeq -> removeLowFRClusters -> baselineFR`.\n- Reference analyses commonly use `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`, which matches the decoder task requirement.\n- Spike data are not converted to dF/F; these are electrophysiology sessions. Neural activity is processed as binned/smoothed firing rates.\n- Neural binning is configurable. Core loaders support arbitrary `dt`; multiple figure scripts use `dt = 1/100` (10 ms) and some use `dt = 1/200` (5 ms). Final choice must be reconciled with paper/methods and data in later steps.\n- `getSeq` bins spikes on `[tmin, tmax)` using `histc`, then smooths counts converted to Hz with `mySmooth`. Session-level single-trial neural data end up as `(time, neurons, trials)`.\n- Cluster curation has two layers:\n  - Quality filter by label using `findClusters`.\n  - Firing-rate filter using `removeLowFRClusters`, typically `lowFR = 1` Hz in several figure scripts and tutorial code.\n- Video/behavior streams are interpolated to the neural/session time axis rather than used at native frame rate.\n- Motion energy alignment is explicit: `interp1(frameTimes - video_offset - align_event_time, me.data{trial}, obj.time + advance_movement)`, then edge NaNs are filled with nearest values.\n- DLC trajectories also use frame times corrected by a 0.5 s or computed video offset depending on loader/helper path. This is important for later consistency checks on temporal alignment.\n- Trial selection is driven by expressions over `obj.bp` fields such as `R`, `L`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `autowater`, `stim.enable`, and `early`.\n- `autowater` is the code’s proxy for behavioral context: delayed response / 2AFC (`autowater == 0`) versus water-cued (`autowater == 1`).\n- Choice/context decoding scripts in `code/ChoiceContextDecoding` balance trials across classes, typically use hit/miss subsets, and operate on `obj.trialdat` or interpolated kinematic tensors aligned to go cue.\n- Metadata loaders encode the included sessions and which probe corresponds to ALM. Some sessions have one relevant probe and some analyses can concatenate two probes within a session.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- Top-level raw-data folders:\n  - `data/Ephys_Behavior`: delayed-response / water-cued sessions with neural data and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n  - `data/RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior`: randomized-delay sessions; most have neural data plus separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n  - `data/DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior`: behavior-only inhibition sessions; no neural `clu` field, motion energy embedded inside `obj.me`.\n  - `data/GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior`: behavior-only inhibition sessions; no neural `clu` field.\n- File format:\n  - All raw session files are MATLAB `.mat`.\n  - Most `data_structure_*.mat` files are MATLAB v7.3/HDF5; at least the older `EKH1` and `EKH3` sessions are non-HDF5 MATLAB files.\n  - `motionEnergy_*.mat` files load directly with SciPy and contain `me.data` and `me.moveThresh`.\n- Raw session object layout:\n  - Ephys sessions: `obj.bp`, `obj.clu`, `obj.ex`, `obj.meta`, `obj.pth`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.traj`, `obj.trials`.\n  - Behavior-only sessions: `obj.bp`, `obj.ex`, `obj.me`, `obj.pth`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.traj`, `obj.trials`.\n- Important raw variables observed:\n  - `obj.bp`: trial labels (`L`, `R`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `autowater`, `early`, `protocol`, `stim`).\n  - `obj.bp.ev`: event times including `bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL`, `lickR`.\n  - `obj.clu`: per-probe spike data with fields `quality`, `site`, `spkWavs`, `tm`, `trial`, `trialtm`.\n  - `obj.traj`: two camera views; each view stores per-trial lists of `frameTimes`, `ts`, `featNames`, etc.\n  - External motion-energy files: `me.data` is one trace per trial; `moveThresh` is a per-session threshold.\n- Representative dimensions from raw files:\n  - DLC trajectory arrays are `(n_frames, 3, n_features)` per trial.\n  - Example side-camera trial: `(2639, 3, 7)`; example bottom-camera trial: `(2639, 3, 10)`.\n  - Example video sampling interval is about `2.52 ms` (`~396.8 Hz`).\n  - Example motion-energy trial length is `2639` samples for an ephys session, consistent with the video frame count.\n- Raw-data caveats found directly from the files:\n  - `data_structure_JEB24_2023-10-03.mat` and `data_structure_JEB24_2023-10-04.mat` live in `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior` but have no `clu` field, so they are behavior-only for decoder purposes.\n  - Those same two JEB24 sessions are also missing external `motionEnergy_*.mat` companions.\n  - Probe location metadata are inconsistently populated in the raw files; some sessions record `L ALM` / `R ALM`, many store no location string.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 14,297 raw clusters across sessions with neural data |\n| Neurons / session | mean 317.7, median 70, min 17, max 1258 (raw, pre-curation) |\n| Subjects | 14 mice with neural data; 18 mice total across all raw folders |\n| Sessions / subject | mean 3.21 neural sessions per mouse (range 1-10) |\n| Trials (total) | 15,324 trials in sessions with neural data; 37,073 trials across all raw folders |\n| Trials / session | mean 340.5, median 343, min 230, max 517 for neural-data sessions |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | Two-context task: 522 units; block structure is described | “approximately 100 DR trials” at session start, then “10–25 trials” per interleaved block |\n| Neural data time bin | 5 ms in published decoding analyses | “each bin is 5 ms” |\n| Behavior data time bin | 400 Hz raw video, approximately 2.5 ms per frame; decoding analyses also operate on 5 ms bins | “High-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate)” and “each bin is 5 ms” |\n| Reward rate | Reward delivered for correct DR choices; WC reward is approximately 3 µl at a random port | “water reward was delivered” / “an approximately 3-µl water reward was presented randomly” |\n| Task/behavior statistic 1 | Fixed-delay DR performance criterion: >70% accuracy before/for expert animals | “reached at least 70% accuracy”; “became experts (>70% accuracy …)” |\n| Task/behavior statistic 2 | Randomized-delay early-lick criterion: <20% | “became experts (>70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate)” |\n| Task/behavior statistic 3 | Behavioral analysis sessions required at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction | “at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction… and 20 correct WC trials for each direction” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task structure:\n  - DR task: 1.3 s auditory sample, then delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue.\n  - Standard fixed-delay DR task used 0.9 s delay for 12 mice; one mouse had 0.7 s delay that was linearly time-warped to 0.9 s in analyses.\n  - WC task omits auditory cues; a water drop appears at a random side/time.\n  - Two-context sessions begin with about 100 DR trials, then alternate WC/DR blocks of 10-25 trials; sessions begin in DR.\n  - Randomized-delay task uses six delay values: 0.3, 0.6, 1.2, 1.8, 2.4, 3.6 s.\n- Temporal alignment:\n  - Methods and figure text repeatedly describe activity, movement, and decoding relative to the go cue (or water drop in WC plots); the user task specifically requires go-cue alignment, matching the main neural alignment used in the code and paper.\n  - ITI context analyses use the 300 ms before sample onset.\n- Kinematic/video processing:\n  - Two 400 Hz cameras.\n  - Tongue, jaw, nose tracked in both views; paws tracked from bottom view only.\n  - Use x/y position and velocity per tracked feature.\n  - Missing values are filled with nearest values for all features except the tongue.\n  - Tongue angle and length are derived from the bottom camera.\n- Motion energy processing:\n  - Frame-wise motion energy is based on the absolute difference between the median of the next five frames and previous five frames.\n  - Each frame is summarized by the 99th percentile across pixels.\n  - Move/non-move threshold is set manually per session from bimodal distributions.\n- Decoding in the paper:\n  - Equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials were used for choice decoding.\n  - Separate models were trained at each time bin for neural and kinematic decoding.\n  - Models used ridge regularization and four-fold cross-validation.\n  - Chance accuracy was computed by shuffling labels.\n- Neural recording/processing:\n  - ALM extracellular recordings from H2 and Neuropixels 1.0 probes.\n  - Spike sorting via JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 with manual Phy curation.\n  - Sessions were included only if they had at least 10 units.\n  - For most analyses, all units with firing rate >1 Hz were included.\n  - For subspace alignment and some single-unit analyses, only well-isolated single units with firing rate >1 Hz were included.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Manual spike-sorting curation distinguishes well-isolated single units from multiunits.\n- Recording sessions must have at least 10 units to be analyzed.\n- A >1 Hz firing-rate threshold is applied.\n- Some analyses use only well-isolated single units >1 Hz; other analyses include all units >1 Hz.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Early-lick trials are omitted from analyses.\n- Ignore / no-response trials are omitted from behavioral analyses.\n- Behavioral analyses require enough correct trials per class: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction.\n- Choice decoding uses equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice from uninstructed movement / neural activity | Reported qualitatively as above chance over time; exact numeric curve values are shown graphically, not tabulated in text |\n| Context from kinematics / neural activity | Reported qualitatively as above chance over time, including during ITI; exact numeric curve values are shown graphically, not tabulated in text |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Randomized-delay session count | `Figure3i.m` loads JEB11 (2), JEB12 (2), JEB23 (7; Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `obj.clu{probe}.trialtm`, `obj.clu{probe}.trial`, `obj.bp.ev.goCue` | `neural` | Align each spike to go cue, bin at 5 ms from `-2.5` to `+2.5` s, smooth with the reference kernel, convert to firing rate, arrange as `(neurons, time)` per trial | `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `removeLowFRClusters` | Use the probe(s) selected by the reference loader files; concatenate probes within a session if code does so. |\n| Common aligned time axis | `input[0]` | Continuous time-from-go-cue series, same for every trial, shape `(1, time)` | `getSeq` / paper decoding methods | Input name: `time_from_go_cue`. |\n| `obj.bp.R` / `obj.bp.L` | `output[0]` | Per-trial categorical label: left=`0`, right=`1` | `findTrials`, task definitions in methods | Exclude early and no-response trials so choice/outcome are well-defined. |\n| `obj.bp.autowater` | `output[1]` | Per-trial categorical label remapped to WC=`0`, DR=`1` | `findTrials`; paper/task description | Raw code uses `autowater==1` as WC. |\n| `obj.bp.hit` / `obj.bp.miss` | `output[2]` | Per-trial categorical label: miss=`0`, hit=`1` | `getOutcome` | Ignore/no-response trials excluded rather than forced into incorrect. |\n| DLC trajectories for tongue-related features | `output[3]` | Reference kinematic interpolation and velocity computation, then aggregate to a single tongue-speed trace and bin by per-session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematics` | Because the decoder spec needs one tongue-velocity output, aggregate across tongue velocity channels after reference processing. |\n| DLC trajectories for paw-related features | `output[4]` | Reference kinematic interpolation and velocity computation, then aggregate to a single paw-speed trace and bin by per-session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematics` | Paws are tracked in the bottom view only. |\n| Motion energy traces (`motionEnergy_*.mat` or embedded `obj.me`) | `output[5]` | Align/interpolate to neural time axis using reference logic, then bin by per-session median | `loadMotionEnergy`, `loadMotionEnergy_Behav` | The paper’s manual move threshold is not used because the decoder task explicitly asks for 50th-percentile binning. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session inclusion**: Include only neural sessions represented in the reference ephys loaders.\n   Fixed-delay set: all 25 sessions in `Ephys_Behavior`.\n   Randomized-delay set: the 19-session subset encoded by `loadJEB11_ALMVideo`, `loadJEB12_ALMVideo`, `loadJEB23_ALMVideo`, and `loadJEB24_ALMVideo`.\n   Behavior-only inhibition sessions are excluded because they have no neural activity.\n2. **Trial inclusion**: Use only control/non-stimulation trials with valid behavioral labels.\n   Exclude `stim.enable`, `early`, and `no` trials to match the reference analyses and keep output definitions unambiguous.\n3. **Neural curation**: Apply the released-code quality filter (`all` except garbage/noisy-like labels), then remove units with firing rate `<= 1 Hz`, and exclude sessions with fewer than 10 remaining units.\n4. **Temporal representation**: Use go-cue alignment and a common 5 ms bin width over `[-2.5 s, +2.5 s)`.\n   This follows the paper’s explicit decoding-bin specification while preserving the baseline window used in the methods and the code.\n5. **Context encoding**: Represent behavioral context as WC=`0`, DR=`1`, even though raw `autowater` uses the opposite polarity.\n6. **Outcome encoding**: Represent only miss versus hit.\n   Ignore trials are removed instead of being merged into incorrect.\n7. **Kinematic scalar outputs**: After reproducing the reference video interpolation and per-feature velocity calculation, collapse the multi-feature representation to scalar tongue-speed and paw-speed traces using aggregate speed across the relevant tracked points.\n   This is a required deviation from the reference feature set because the decoder task asks for exactly one tongue-velocity output and one paw-velocity output.\n8. **Motion-energy binning**: Use the reference aligned continuous trace, but discretize with the task-mandated per-session median instead of the paper’s manually chosen move threshold.\n9. **Brain-region labels**: Store per-neuron labels from the raw selected probe metadata, preserving hemisphere-specific ALM labels and any additional locations present in pooled sessions; fall back to `ALM` only when the raw location string is missing.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot-check: for selected session/trial/neuron, verify converted binned firing rates match raw spike times aligned to go cue and
